## Rect coverage agrees at half edges and misses at quarter-left edges (2026-09-28 decision analysis)
Question: does the edge-quantum residual live in the coverage kernel or the outline path, and what rounding rule explains it?
Method: two admitted-syntax 16x16 black rect fills (x 10.5 and x 10.25, width 5) with no product change; default render; fresh triples 3/3 identical; per-pixel sampling of oracle against candidate.
Facts: half edges exact at 0 pixels with 128 against 128 on both half-covered columns. Quarter edges miss 12 pixels at max error 1 on column 10 only: nominal 0.75 coverage renders 191 here against 192 in Chrome, while nominal 0.25 coverage agrees at 64 and full coverage at 255. No rounding rule maps input 191.25 to 192, so Chrome effective coverage there is not 0.75; uniform edge expansion is refuted by bounds (needs plus 0.0029 left but at most 0.0010 right). The divergence is positional or coverage-formula, not output rounding, and the outline Family A residuals share its one-level signature.
Decision: the quarter-edge probe stays as an admittedRendering ratchet of 12 pixels at max error 1; the exact half-edge probe leaves no manifest entry. Next: S11 resolves against Skia sources which of edge position, coverage formula, or quantum bias explains the 0.75-only disagreement.

## Skia edge alpha truncates coverage times 256; ours rounds times 255 (2026-09-28 source analysis)
Question: what quantization rule explains 191 here against 192 in Chrome at nominal 0.75 coverage, with agreement at 0.25, 0.5 and 1.0?
Method: DEPS revalidation against the browser pin (cr-rev maps Chromium 1681091 to main commit 86cee6df; its DEPS pins skia 8b8c3872, which the retained f8b66b7 record does not match); blobless sparse checkout of that revision, read-only under external/skia and unreferenced; read the SkScan_AAAPath trapezoid alpha functions, the SkBlitter fat-rect route, the SkDraw rect routing, and our WithCoverage.
Facts: Skia trapezoid_to_alpha truncates SkFixed area right by 8 (floor of coverage times 256, full coverage special-cased to 255 through fixed_to_alpha) while ours rounds Alpha times coverage half-away (times 255). Predictions fit all four sampled anchors: 0.75 gives 192 against 191, 0.25 gives 64 against 64, 0.5 gives 128 against 128, 1.0 gives 255 against 255. The blitFatAntiRect scalar route would give 191 and is excluded by the oracle; filled AA rects route through SkScan AntiFillRect into the analytic mask machinery. Integer-edge exactness is rule-invariant since both rules agree at 0 and 1, so a switch only moves fractional-coverage pixels.
Decision: the quantum rule is solved as specified; applying floor256 with the 255 special case is an S11 calibration change (hot rasterizer plus full re-probe plus manifest updates) for its owner, expected low-risk by the invariance argument and verified by re-probe, not by assertion. Next: reconcile the retained DEPS record against 8b8c3872; family-B geometry is untouched by this rule.

## Quantum rule holds on vertical edges with transposed signature (2026-09-28 decision analysis)

## Diagonal coverage differs by Skia fixed-point construction, not precision (2026-09-28 source analysis)

Exact analytic area therefore cannot agree with this walk: the residual is structural, and integer vertices rule out position quantization as the source.
Decision: diagonal exactness is unreachable by improving analytic precision; it needs bug-compatible replication of the Skia fixed-point walk or a negotiated fidelity target, for S11. Next: no further diagonal probes until one of those two routes opens; the X ratchet pins the current delta.

Method: two admitted-syntax 16x16 probes (band stroked rect, userSpace red-blue stroked line) with no product change; default render; fresh triples 3/3 identical; VisualDiff.
Facts: both byte-exact. Band lowering with mitered band corners agrees, and userSpace gradient evaluation on lines adds zero error as claimed.
Decision: no probes retained since exact renders have no manifest home, and no product change. Next: nothing open on bands or gradient line strokes.

## Floor256 switch work order: six sites, contained blast radius (2026-09-28 decision analysis)
Question: what exactly does applying the floor256 quantum rule touch, and how big is the blast radius?
Method: census of coverage-to-alpha quantization sites across src with no product change.
Facts: six sites quantize coverage or alpha with round-half behavior: WithCoverage (SvgConverter, coverage times alpha rounded half away), BlendSourceOver transparent branch (plus 127 over 255 thrice), ComposeFractionalChannel retained branch (half-outputAlpha and 255-times-outputAlpha-minus-one biases), and three identical MultiplyAlpha copies adding 127 over 255 in SvgConverter, SvgPaintResolver and SvgReferenceResolver. Exact entries stay exact under the switch since both rules agree at coverages 0 and 1; only fractional-coverage pixels move, which are exactly the ratcheted residual sets plus gated rejections.
Decision: the switch is a sequenced S11 calibration change (quantization sites, twin tests per affected path, full re-probe, manifest and research ratchet regeneration) for its owner, not a solo turn; containment follows from integer-edge invariance. Next: no partial switch, which would mix rules across stages.

## Miter threshold now tests the full displacement ratio (2026-09-28 implementation)
Question: does EmitJoin compare the emitted miter tip consistently with the caller limit.
Method: algebra of the emitted tip (halfWidth times (n1+n2) over (1+dot)) against the tested ratio, checked against the release-tag Skia MiterJoiner inverse-limit comparison; new limit-2 branch regression on the (0,0) to (5,0) to (1,3) turn whose true ratio is sqrt(10).
Facts: the old comparison halved the displacement length, so any limit in [true/2, true) wrongly mitered; the new test bevels at limit 2 while limit 1 still bevels and limit 4 still miters, and the new test fails on the old code. A full 66-probe sweep after the fix moves only research-miter-fallback-acute-16x16, from 50 pixels at max error 255 to 46 at 36 with bbox x1..8 y1..13, a strict improvement on the default-limit acute witness. The core suite holds 2497 green with no exact-fixture change.
Decision: the branch repair is retained with its regression and updated ratchet. Remaining spike-edge residuals stay under diagonal coverage and bevel-cut work, not miter math. Next: R41 degenerate gradient strokes, then row 2.

## Degenerate objectBoundingBox gradient strokes paint nothing (2026-09-28 implementation)
Question: why the R41 horizontal gradient path stroke paints black while Chrome paints none.
Method: traced the stroke repaint box (centerline bounds, zero height) into gradient polygon evaluation, where division by the zero box height yields non-finite normalized coordinates that interpolate to black; pre/post MemoryProbe renders with a stash-reverted before check across a five-case matrix.
Facts: the pre-fix candidate differs from the post-fix one by exactly the 24 black pixels at x2..13 y7..8 recorded in the review; post-fix horizontal, vertical, radial and use-instance candidates have PNG alpha-sum 0, while the userSpaceOnUse control paints the 24-pixel band (alpha-sum 6120). The full gate holds 2502 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font green with no fixture or research manifest change.
Decision: the shared rasterizer guard is retained in both gradient polygon rasterizers with userSpace paints exempt; the line-spelling rejection boundary is unchanged and no parser-level box rewrite was needed. Next: row 2.

## Solid, gradient and radial fills share one contour preparation path (2026-09-28 implementation)
Question: can the three duplicated validate, classify, split and signs pipelines share one owned contour boundary without changing behavior.
Method: extracted PrepareSeparatedContours returning a SeparatedContours payload (points, ends, signs, mapping); FinishPolygonFill, FinishGradientPolygonFill and FinishRadialPolygonFill keep only their paint-specific Retain calls with identical operation, charging and cancellation order. Full solution gate plus memory-probe scenarios serve as the behavior-preservation evidence.
Facts: net minus 52 lines in SvgOrthogonalGeometryLowerer with zero warning or error; gate counts identical to row-1 completion at 2502 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font with no fixture or research movement; all memory scenarios pass. FinishStrokeFill stays separate since it lifts zero roles without structure splitting, and reserve-before-allocate transfer reservations stay open.
Decision: the shared contour boundary is retained as the first row-2 slice. Next: transfer-boundary reservations with ownership, then raster-loop sharing through the same payload.

## Polygon retention reserves scene storage before allocating copies (2026-09-28 implementation)
Question: can the shared contour boundary reserve its retained arrays before allocating them, with ownership transferring into the fill.
Method: PrepareSeparatedContours now pre-reserves exact Scene bytes for points, ends and signs, re-reserves exact bytes after crossing splits while the split lists are still held, and carries the reservation in SeparatedContours into Create, which adopts it instead of reserving again; all other Create callers pass null and keep current behavior. Operation, charging and cancellation order are unchanged. Three pre-reserve denial tests, one exact Scene-bytes transfer test, two resized crossing-split denial tests, the full gate and the memory scenarios serve as evidence.
Facts: denial now precedes retained copies on all three shared paths, and the single-crossing split denial still fires at the split-ends list with the pre-reservation held, covering simultaneous old/new storage under denial. The gate holds 2506 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font with no fixture or research movement, and all memory scenarios pass. Ends arrays allocated upstream in MapDiagonalContours are covered by catch-up accounting at Prepare; threading that reservation from the mapper stays the next ownership slice, as does FinishStrokeFill.
Decision: the transfer boundary is retained for the three shared fill paths. Next: mapper-side ends reservations, then raster-loop sharing through the same payload.

## Stroke retention joins the pre-reserved transfer boundary (2026-09-28 implementation)
Question: does the fourth contour owner close the retention transfer boundary without changing stroke behavior.
Method: FinishStrokeFill now pre-reserves exact Scene bytes, copies under cover, and transfers the reservation through RetainPolygonFill into Create adoption; a hand-sized stroke denial test plus the full gate and memory scenarios serve as evidence.
Facts: denial precedes retained and role copies on the stroke path with full release, and the gate holds 2507 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font with no fixture or research movement. The use and symbol remap paths already reserve transient mapped storage before copying and only share source ends and signs conservatively, so they need no change. Remaining transfer work is the upstream mapper ends reservation and any pattern-polygon retention, which keeps its own Create path.
Decision: all four polygon fill retention paths now share reserve-before-allocate with ownership transfer. Next: raster-loop sharing through the same payload, then row 3 calibration.

## Four polygon rasterizers share one mapping path (2026-09-28 implementation)
Question: can the four polygon mapping phases share one boundary without changing pixels or denial behavior.
Method: extracted a MapPolygon local function returning an owned MappedPolygon payload (points, bounds, clip, fractional flag, surface reservation); the solid, linear, radial and pattern loops keep only paint evaluation. Denial order is unchanged since charging, reservation and cancellation points moved verbatim. The full gate, six-output parity tests and memory scenarios serve as evidence.
Facts: net minus 76 lines in SvgConverter with zero warnings; gate holds 2509 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font with no fixture or research movement, and all memory scenarios pass. Per-pixel coverage dispatch is shared through a local evaluator; paint evaluation stays per loop by design: unifying it would need per-pixel polymorphism on the hot path, and row 4 replaces the fan-scan traversal anyway.
Decision: the shared mapping boundary is retained and completes the row-2 consolidation. Next: mark row 2 complete and begin row 3 calibration through its narrow work order.

## Translucent and half-coverage quantum controls agree exactly (2026-09-28 implementation)
Question: do the missing row-3 control cells (translucent 127/128, half coverage, black background) agree with Chrome or bound the calibration.
Method: five admitted-syntax 16x16 quarter and half-edge rect probes (opaque half, alpha-127 and 128 quarters, black-background quarter, alpha-128 half) with three fresh-process captures each, candidate renders and decoded VisualDiff comparison, plus non-vacuity cross-checks against the opaque anchor.
Facts: all five triples byte-stable and all five candidates byte-exact, so no research ratchets were owed; all five graduate to exact fixtures as original-quantum pins (336 entries). The only standing quantum residuals are the opaque 0.75 anchors (191 against 192, transparent and white). Translucent composition therefore already agrees and the calibration scope narrows to opaque mask quantization.
Decision: no product change in this slice; independently useful successes are retained as exact fixtures rather than deleted. Next: the narrowed opaque-mask rule with these five as must-stay-exact guards.

## Tenth-coverage adjudication refutes pure rules and finds path-band jitter (2026-09-28 decision analysis)
Question: does a 0.1-coverage edge adjudicate between floor-quantization and position-offset accounts of the quantum residuals.
Method: three admitted-syntax 16x16 fractional path probes (opaque, alpha-127 and alpha-128 at left 0.1 coverage) with three fresh-process captures each, per-pixel decoded comparison against candidates, plus column profiles of both sides.
Facts: all triples stable. Chrome reads 25 at the opaque left edge and 229 at the right against our 26 and 230, killing the position-offset account; alpha-127 reads 12 against 13 while alpha-128 is exact. No fixed mask-plus-combination rule fits all ten pinned cells (a monotone byte rule cannot send 95.62 to 95 and 31.88 to 32), and no global offset fits edges needing opposite signs, which favors phase-dependent fixed-point truncation noise as row 4 already routes. Separately, our fractional path bands jitter per row (25/26 and 229/230 scattered) while Chrome is uniform and our rectangle bands are uniform: per-pixel fan coverage carries float noise across rows, a spelling-dependent defect owned by row 4 spatial work, not by calibration.
Decision: opaque tenth-edge and translucent-127 tenth-edge stay as admittedRendering ratchets of 12 pixels at max error 1 each; translucent-128 tenth-edge graduates as an exact fixture. No byte-rule product change: none is supported by evidence. Next: row-4 matched fixed-point scan conversion, which owns both the truncation model and the fan jitter.

## Row-4 scoped plan: spatial membership over intervals (2026-09-28 planning)
Question: what is the ordered, bounded route from the current fan scans to spatial winding and matched straight-edge conversion.
Method: mechanism inventory from retained oracle evidence: per-row fan float noise straddling exact half boundaries on straight edges, fringe doubling on duplicate strokes, uniform bands versus jittered fans on identical geometry, and zero-lifting role repair already landed. Fresh re-measurement of the review stroke pair under the current tree reproduces 50 pixels at max error 8 single and 50 at 108 duplicate with identical reference hashes, so the premises hold post row-1 and row-2.
Facts: the duplicate pair stays as admittedRendering ratchets of 50 at 8 and 50 at 108 with bboxes x1..14 y1..14. Ordered slices: first, duplicate and overlapping stroke acceptance (investigate per-subpath separation versus spatial union before changing compositing); second, straight-edge determinism through a matched fixed-point construction with the quarter, half and tenth anchors plus translucent guards as acceptance; third, an interval winding and parity resolver for touching, coincident, nested and crossing contours at the topology level; fourth, full traversal replacement through the shared payload with work charging and during-phase cancellation. Each input keeps comparison against its own reference since the browser itself deltas single versus duplicate by 28 pixels at max error 5. No fixture-specific deduplication or per-pixel correction is acceptable.
Decision: this plan replaces the missing R34 spatial stage and absorbs the redirected row-3 quantum work. Next: the duplicate-stroke investigation slice.

## Duplicate-stroke doubling localized to unsigned contour summation (2026-09-28 investigation)
Question: where does the duplicate-stroke fringe doubling come from, and is per-subpath separation or spatial union the fix.
Method: geometry dump through the internal parse seam for both retained probes, plus the pinned oracle deltas. No product change.
Facts: the single stroke lowers to one PolygonFill with 4 points, ends [4] and roles [1]; the duplicate lowers to one fill with 8 points, ends [4,8] and roles [1,1]. The roles rasterizer sums unsigned per-contour fan areas, so full pixels clamp identically while fringe partials double (0.3 plus 0.3 renders 0.6), which is exactly the retained 50 pixels at max error 108 against 8 for the single. Chrome deltas single versus duplicate by 28 pixels at max error 5, so it double-coats rather than unions: neither our sum nor a pure union reproduces its mechanism, though union bounds the error at Chrome self-delta scale. Per-subpath separation would reproduce double-coating faithfully but splits every multi-contour scene into sequential composites with work, memory and draw-order blast radius; clamping per contour or taking same-sign maxima mispaints disjoint islands sharing a pixel and is rejected. Fixture-specific deduplication is rejected by plan.
Decision: no product change in this slice. The fix belongs to the interval-winding slice with true union semantics, guarded by the retained 50 at 8 and 50 at 108 ratchets with per-input references. Next: straight-edge determinism through the matched fixed-point construction.

## Circle sampling direction flipped; dash phase and length remain open (2026-09-28 implementation)
Question: does flipping closed-loop sampling direction close the dashed-circle gap, and what remains.
Method: SampleFull sweeps negative angles from the same origin; solid-circle pre/post renders compared byte-identical; offset sweep plus ring-profile alignment in both orientations; single-dash and aperiodic probes with fresh triples; full fixture and research re-measurement with before/after tuples.
Facts: the flip is safe (solid circle identical) and net positive across the dash family (butt 94 to 90, offset 98 to 92 with max error 255 to 241, circle-dash mark 114 to 103 with max alpha 240 to 232, circle-dash-offset mark 113 to 106). The ellipse-dash mark regresses (88 to 93 with max alpha 223 to 255) and is recorded as such. No rotation, reflection or offset of our pattern aligns the single-dash oracle (best 76 at max error 175 with 20-plus mismatches both ways), and our dash sits off the predicted arc while the sampled contour starts a tessellation step past the path origin, so start phase, true-length metric and seam merging stay open as separate follow-ups with the three new probes as acceptance. Completion in the same slice: the fourth mover (ellipse-dash-offset 86 to 77 with max alpha 226 to 123) was re-measured with a hash-matched candidate, a third probe (ellipse single-dash, 54 pixels from a fresh identical triple) was admitted, and the four fixture reason strings were reconciled to the measured tuples.
Decision: the flip is retained with updated ratchets and three new probes; the remaining dash work is start phase, arc-length metric and seam merging in that order. Next: row-7 S14 blockers or the dash start-phase slice.

## Circle flip reverted; sampled loops lead with the origin (2026-09-28 implementation)
Question: is the negative-angle traversal correct, and why does the dash start a tessellation step past the path origin.Method: 512px exact-upscale single-dash control with a fresh Chrome capture and MemoryProbe render; side-by-side block map plus quarter-degree angular histograms on the mid-ring band for solid and faint coverage; direction audit of the sampler parametrization against the measured oracle side; edge-set audit of every SampleFull consumer; origin-first rotation of the loop buffer; full fixture and research re-measurement.Facts: the flipped renderer paints the dash on the north side (mid-ring 263 to 356.5 degrees) while Chrome paints the south side (0 to 91.5 degrees) from the shared 3-o-clock start, so the 0-to-negative-2pi sweep mirrors the browser and the flip is reverted; its ratchet deltas were seam-phase luck on non-integer-period circumferences, with ellipse-dash regressing throughout. The loop buffer rotates its closing origin to the front with an identical cyclic edge set, so fills and solid strokes are untouched while dash measurement begins at the path start like open contours. The 512px control falls from 33476 to 794 changed pixels with mid-ring solid and faint coverage exactly (0 to 91.75, 0 to 92) on both renderers, matching the nominal 256-unit true arc, and residuals sit at both dash-end caps plus a start sliver. Ratchets move to research 76, 80, 34, 21 and 21 with maxima 131, 152, 95, 49 and 49, and fixtures 94, 94, 78 and 76; the full gate holds with no exact-corpus change.Decision: the flip is reverted and the origin-first rotation is retained with updated ratchets; dash-end cap orientation and seam merging remain the follow-ups. Next: the dash-end cap slice, row-7 S14 blockers or row-5 affine core.

## Dashed-circle residue is cap orientation, not cut position (2026-09-28 analysis)Question: after the origin fix, what remains of the 794 changed pixels on the 512px single-dash control, and can a polyline-level fix close it.Method: exact Python mirror of the adaptive sampler (flatness 0.25, r 160) giving 64 vertices with a 5.625-degree first step and chord total 1004.906 against true 1005.310; pattern walk replayed in chord space; alpha block maps plus cap windows at both dash ends for Chrome and ours; code-point audit of butt-cap emission.Facts: the replayed dash cut lands at 91.71 degrees against the true-arc 91.673 degrees, so cut position is exact to 0.1px and the deficit is orientation: butt caps emit perpendicular to the terminal chord per the documented S11 flattened-offset boundary, tilted half a step (2.8 degrees, about 3px across the 64px cap) from the true tangent, while Chrome cuts perpendicular to the curve tangent. Mid-segment cuts cannot be reoriented from polylines because every subsegment keeps the chord direction, and secant tangents would change cap emission for all open strokes, so the correct fix is curve-aware run stroking owned by rows 4 and 5. The 16px probes sit at fringe level (21 at 49, 34 at 95) and cannot move on geometry.Decision: recorded as the scoped dash-end cap slice with the 512px control as acceptance; no kernel or densification hack. Next: that slice when rows 4 and 5 reach curve-aware stroking, otherwise row-7 S14 blockers or row-5 affine core.

## Open-stroke stroking is quad-union, not joined polygons (2026-09-28 analysis)Question: why do open-polyline turns mismatch at full-pixel strength, and what stroking model fits every measured pixel.Method: solid butt-run control with a fresh Chrome capture; straight-plus-offset and natural-phase bisections; per-pixel boundary reconstruction across five dash variants; an inner-butt single-contour attempt with revert; closed-ring control.Facts: the solid run mismatches 6 pixels at max 255 with Chrome filling the inner notch our inner miter vertex carves, while straight-plus-offset stays exact at 8 pixels of max 1, proving the offset convention. Every pixel across the variants fits per-segment butt quads plus outer join wedges unioned: inner butt corners plus outer miter corner, with no inner vertex. The inner-butt-plus-jump single contour fills the notch but its diagonal cuts the cap-row neighborhood and it breaks closed-loop hole corners (ring pixel 255 to 128), so it was reverted the same day; closed loops keep sharp inner corners, which quad-union also produces. Quad contacts are interior or point-only, so the summing filler stays safe here, unlike coincident duplicate strokes.Decision: scoped open-stroke quad-plus-wedge rewrite slice for miter and bevel joins with the pathlength, offset and solid-run controls as acceptance; round joins keep current behavior with own probes and closed loops stay untouched. Next: that rewrite, or row-7 S14 blockers or row-5 affine core.

## Open miter joins trace quad union with outer ears (2026-09-28 implementation)Question: how to stroke open miter and round joins to the pixel-complete quad-union model without breaking closed loops or round behavior.Method: EmitJoin gains an open-contour flag; open miter joins close butt on the concave side selected by positive cross with nonnegative dot and spike the outer side through the miter tip, tracing the wedge ear in one contour; round fans move to the outer side; fan chords cap at thirty degrees; outline capacities rise four to six times points for the ear triples; solid-run, V-shape, acute and rounded controls plus full manifest re-measurement.Facts: the pathlength probe falls 5 to 3 with maxima 102 to 25 as the notch fills, the round-stroke mark 59 to 40, the round-join rounded mark 194 to 184 and the round-join-even probe 56 to 49 with maxima 201 to 13, and the closed-loop dash-offset probe graduates exact as the 338th fixture. The dot term is load-bearing: acute inner turns keep the classic vertex, which the acute oracle matches, while right turns within ninety degrees close butt. Tessellation joints boil plus or minus one pixel honestly on curved dashes. Closed loops, bevel branches, spikes and round caps keep their paths; the full gate holds with no exact-corpus change.Decision: retained with updated ratchets and one graduation; round caps and joins share the finer fan. Next: dash-end caps at scale, row-7 S14 blockers or row-5 affine core.

## Diagonal clip application scoped on inv-085 (2026-09-28 analysis)Question: what exactly must clip-path application compute for the saltire clip, and what remains after geometry.Method: exact vertex walk of the clip path; separate renders of ground, white saltire, red saltire and cross; ghost composite with a binary triangle-union mask under source-over against the staged 1000x600 oracle triple; mismatch attribution by distance to clip edges and diagonal bands.Facts: the clip path is four same-wound plus-187.5 triangles touching only at the shared vertex, unioning to half the viewport under the default nonzero rule. All four parts render. The ghost matches except 5762 pixels at max 143, all inside the diagonal-stroke band (4134) or at clip-edge and diagonal intersections (1628) with zero elsewhere, so clip geometry and part correctness are proven and the residual is the standing diagonal-AA family plus binary-mask clip edges. Rectangle-union stays inapplicable. Sound application needs contour intersection with union semantics, touching-vertex handling from row 4, while diagonal edges ride row 4 and S11; the expected outcome is a Czech-like known-difference render, not exact.Decision: scoped clip-application slice with inv-085 rendering at diagonal-edge residual as acceptance; path-child clip registration rides along. Next: row-4 spatial machinery, row-5 affine core, or the dash-end cap slice.

## Coincident contours merge to first occurrence (2026-09-28 implementation)Question: per-subpath separation or spatial union for the duplicate-stroke doubling, without touching compositing.Method: fringe profiles showing exact doubling with clamp on the duplicate probe against single and oracle; combination-point audit through roles into unsigned fan-area summation; alternative analysis (blanket max-union undercounts touching dash seams, signed winding still doubles); bitwise-identical merge helper with pair charging and per-vertex copy accounting wired into both fill preparation sites before roles and reservations.Facts: the duplicate probe falls 50 pixels at max 108 to max 10, matching the single-probe family plus quantum. The merge fires nowhere else in the corpus: full gate holds 2516 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font with no exact-corpus change. Touching, nested and crossing contours keep existing combination; rotated and reversed identicals need spatial identity. Compositing is unchanged.Decision: retained with the updated duplicate ratchet; crossing and partial overlaps stay row-4 spatial-union work. ## Deferred gradient href resolution admits forward references; inv-035 renders (2026-09-29 implementation)

## Shared first vertices can misresolve winding roles (2026-09-29 analysis)
Question: why does the mixed crossing-plus-touch union deviate exactly at the shared pixel (85 vs oracle 170) while every other pixel matches, and is any accumulator wrapping involved. Method: exact per-pixel coverage arithmetic (lobe 127.5 plus triangle 212.5 equals 340.0) against candidate and oracle pixel dumps; code audit of every coverage-to-byte site; instrumental sign read through the parser on the probe. Facts: parsed signs read 1,-1,1: the triangle resolves -1 through its shared first vertex under skip=false while lobe-first vertices stay unshared; Abs masks the sign on singly-covered pixels (triangle-only rows match at 255) but overlap pixels subtract (127.5 minus 212.5 in magnitude gives 85) instead of unioning (170). CoverageAlpha clamps opaque products above 255 and both coverage kernels clamp their totals, and the duplicate probe doubles with clamp, so no wrap site exists and batch-121 overlap-wrap wording is corrected to role subtraction. Fixing the role to +1 would clamp the pixel to 255 (equal and opposite error), so only per-pixel spatial winding (spatial union) closes it; the skip flag stays scoped to resolved sets to avoid destabilizing pinned luck. Decision: recorded with the corrected probe comment; spatial union stays queued. Next: row-4 spatial union, row-5 affine core and tolerance proof, or row-7 clip/mask blockers.

## Vertex-capacity narrowing closed: identical signatures falsify the count hypothesis (2026-09-30 probe work)

The count hypothesis is therefore falsified. The pinned construction snaps Y to quarter pixels and X to 1/16 with truncation, which is inherently position-dependent, so per-input lean variation is expected fringe-phase behavior in the same class as the documented tip-sliver residuals (whose amplitude scales with fringe length: viewbox carries a ~300px fringe ring vs ~125px). A MaxAnalyticVertices=64 gate was implemented, verified to change nothing for the pair (both 32), and reverted; the committed tree carries no gate.

## Touch-tolerant nesting is the engine core; bitwise skip is the precise gap (2026-09-30 probe work)
Question: what exactly blocks the four simplicity rejects, and what is the smallest engine step.

Facts: every single-defect class renders (overlap, edge-share, vertex touch, bowtie, evenodd overlap on straight contours, tangent circles, coincident curved duplicates, cusps, circle-triangle overlap and touch, overlapping strokes). First-failure signatures: inv-001 is 3 contours with touch plus coincident overlap (nonzero, gradient fill); inv-063 is 1 contour with crossing plus coincident overlap plus self-touch (nonzero); inv-065 is 2 contours with one proper crossing (evenodd); inv-082 is 1 contour with one proper crossing (nonzero). The minimal mixed probe is a rect plus triangle sharing one crossing plus one corner-touch (`M8,0 L16,0 L16,8 L8,8 Z M4,4 L12,4 L4,12 Z`, solid nonzero): its pairs render in isolation but the triple rejects. Root cause for touches: TryFindUnsharedTestVertex skips only bitwise-shared vertices, so a vertex lying on another loop edge without a vertex there (the m23 corner) feeds an arbitrary-boundary pip test; the guard therefore must keep rejecting until the skip becomes on-boundary-aware. Stripping one zero-length vertex cleans the 001 arch to no flags, but lowering already strips, so that artifact does not reach the classifier.
Decision: no product change. Next: touch-tolerant nesting (on-boundary-excluding test-vertex search mirroring the clip disjointness pattern, guard tolerance for single-point contacts, union-flag emit) with m23-class synthetic acceptance; evenodd crossings need parity classification by fragment-midpoint and original-vertex strict tests on top.

Facts: shared vertices show alternating L1L2L1L2 incident edges, X-crossings through shared vertices that the proper-crossing finder cannot see and the guard refuses as partial overlap. Same-wound uncontained pairs split at every shared vertex; arcs strictly inside the other loop drop as covered while boundary arcs link deterministically, preferring other-loop traversals with forward chaining that preserves parent orientation; tangent weavings with four boundary ends at one vertex, opposite-wound pairs, contained pairs, zero-area loops, repeated shared values and degenerate arcs stay rejected, and each firing drops at least one interior arc while linking creates no vertices, so the sweep cannot revisit the same pair. Both letter pairs render with fringe-level oracle residuals against banked 3/3-stable triples (n56f oracle SHA-256 1a4122b51c65440c0279c6962144949fff40f9a571fff9e1efa9b2782f6daacb: 151 pixels with only 2 above channel error 40; n1718f oracle SHA-256 6b29206e9d131103df7670a302cc6f87df6314ea07c331ac49f4d7a9ecf1713f: 168 pixels at maximum channel error 87 with maximum alpha error 68; pinned Chrome 153.0.8010.52 recipe 3), reproduced bit-for-bit from a clean rebuild. Both new union tests fail on the clean tree with the honest simplicity rejection. Full 92-document re-probe shows zero disposition drift with byte-identical renders (83 render plus 9 reject). Full gate holds 2763 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font green with zero warnings; memory and package checks pass with zero .svg in the package.
Decision: landed shared-vertex arc linking with honest tangent-weaving boundaries. Next: larger shared-vertex cascades (triple 18,19,20 and the full wordmark path), dense stroked-curve sets (inv-065, inv-082), and stroke unions per the queue.

## Adjacent shared spans splice as chains in one walk jump (2026-10-01 slice)
Question: which gate strands same-wound loop pairs that share an opposite-directed edge chain, with single-edge splice needing exactly one span and multi-span splice needing vertex-disjoint spans.
Method: trace the n181920 triple through the mixed sweep with temporary stderr dumps (reverted before commit); dump loop sizes, shared vertices, winding signs, containment and the boundary-arc walk failure site; hand-trace the remainder walk over the chain.
Facts: the triple settles to three loops (34, 9 and 18 points; areas +434, -35 and +234): loop 1 is an opposite-wound hole strictly inside loop 0 while loop 2 shares the consecutive chain A-B-C with loop 0 in opposite directions (L0 A-B-C, L2 C-B-A) with a 16-edge outer arc and a 2-edge interior arc. Single-edge splice needs exactly one shared span and multi-span splice vetoes the shared middle vertex B, so neither fires; the shared-vertex linker then classifies both chain edges as on-boundary rather than strictly inside and its walk dead-ends arriving at C with no unvisited boundary arc. The remainder walk needs no veto: arriving at the chain-entry vertex it jumps to the other loop unshared edge starting there and skips the covered chain on both sides, covering every remainder edge once into one ring (L0 32-edge plus L2 16-edge remainders), with ambiguous or dead-end continuations still failing honestly and the clean-contour check plus split-loop guard still validating. The veto is removed with no other code change. The triple renders with fringe-level oracle residuals against a fresh banked 3/3-stable triple (oracle SHA-256 2123ee1130dbab483086eef62ccb85f3dcb5f9b3f9a332c04d9022b7972dd5c0: 151 pixels at maximum channel error 86 with maximum alpha error 60, 6 above 40, all verified on curve-edge fringe; pinned Chrome 153.0.8010.52 recipe 3). The full 34-subpath wordmark cascades to admission and renders its solid-fill probe at 2131 pixels at maximum channel error 96 with maximum alpha error 67 (223 above 40, 199 verified edge-fringe with the rest sub-fringe dust or mid-alpha edge shades; oracle SHA-256 cf8b7f7d0329ab12bf061c62abc960a9d63bb490c9979997670892d185f47635, fresh 3/3-stable). The new chain-union test fails on the clean tree with the honest simplicity rejection. Full 92-document re-probe flips inv-003 to a known-difference render with zero other drift and byte-identical renders elsewhere (83 identical, 8 rejects with identical codes). Full gate holds 2764 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font green with zero warnings; memory and package checks pass with zero .svg in the package.
Decision: landed chain splicing by veto removal with honest walk-failure boundaries. Next: swoosh paint fidelity for the inv-003 full-document residuals (row 7), dense stroked-curve sets (inv-065, inv-082), and stroke unions per the queue.

## Swoosh paint evaluates exactly in the interior; residuals are curve-edge coverage (2026-10-01 probe work)

Method: isolate the p0 swoosh path with its gradient defs and document transform in a probe shell; bank a fresh 3/3-stable Chrome triple (oracle SHA-256 98f30cf6fcf56300bdbc7de282808618809cab6d32e595c5b991e34c9f4355f3, pinned Chrome 153.0.8010.52 recipe 3, 738x371); diff candidate versus swoosh-only oracle plus a both-opaque interior analysis.

## Dense-curve work denials are set-level fan charges; stroke outlines need analytic eligibility (2026-10-01 probe work)
Question: which exact mechanism denies inv-082 (LimitExceeded) when every subunit renders alone.
Method: bisect inv-082 into per-path probes preserving group stroke and fill context (29 units: 2 grounds plus 27 dragon paths); split fill-only versus stroke-only on the failing unit; trace analytic eligibility, point counts and bboxes at the coverage-charge site; dump lowering loop counts and ClassifySet flags at the raster payload.
Facts: 26 of 29 units render; d06, d07 and d27 each deny with Polygon fill raster work exceeds the configured limit. d06 lowers to a single self-crossing loop (1 loop, 1128 flattened points, flags ProperCrossing, accepted by the guard) which routes fan-only, so the charge is 1128 points times 234k bbox pixels, about 264M against the 100M ledger. d07 shares the same charge site over 16 subpaths. d27 holds 13 whisker strokes that each render alone, so the denial is purely the set-level pixels-times-points charge on the union. Stroke outlines carry zero roles, so the analytic multi-contour path drops them at the kept-contours filter while the fan combines them as absolute winding clamped to 1; membership agrees (nonzero signed winding if and only if nonzero absolute winding) but edge-AA combination equality through the walker is unproven, and doubled cap edges may add CoincidentOverlap ineligibility on top.
Decision: no product change; the slice is concretely blocked, not abandoned. Return conditions: analytic eligibility for unsigned stroke-outline sets with per-probe oracle fringe-equality proving combination equality (serves d27 and the fan-bound inv-064/inv-086 class), or touching-lobe analytic traversal per the row-4 endgame, or fidelity-safe point reduction for self-crossing fills (serves d06/d07). Next: the stroke-outline eligibility slice, inv-065 matrix composition, or swoosh-adjacent row-7 paint work per the queue.

## Stroke unions bypass resolution with lifted roles; contacts still need the shared scanner (2026-10-01 probe work)
Question: why do the 13 inv-082 whisker strokes deny as a set when each renders alone, and what exactly blocks analytic routing.
Method: split the d27 whisker path into single-subpath probes in identical group context (13 of 13 render); dump loop ends and ClassifySet flags at the solid raster payload (13 loops, 760 points, flags ProperCrossing); read FinishStrokeFill and the fan combination on paper.
Facts: the whisker outlines genuinely cross between loops, and stroke lowering performs no structure validation or crossing resolution by design (spike retraces would false-positive), so the crossing set reaches the rasterizer raw and the set-level pixels-times-points fan charge denies it. Correction to the previous entry: stroke outlines do not carry zero roles — FinishStrokeFill resolves winding roles and lifts zero to plus one for non-degenerate loops so overlapping bands union instead of cancelling, and the fan combines role-weighted (not absolute-clamped) since roles are present. The analytic multi-contour path therefore keeps every +1 contour and refuses only on the contact scan, which rejects crossings, touches, overlaps and self-touches alike. Subdividing the crossings would still leave bitwise-shared vertices, which the same scan refuses: the pinned walker has no touching-set membership, which is exactly the row-4 shared-scanner endgame. Nothing in the charge model can give without weakening denial guarantees, and flattening-point reduction is row-5 fidelity territory.
Decision: no product change. Return condition, refined: shared scan-converter membership over touching sets (active row-4 work order), after which subdivided stroke unions and self-crossing fills route analytic with per-probe oracle fringe-equality. Next: the shared-scanner slice itself, inv-065 matrix composition, or row-7 paint work per the queue.

## Self-crossing fills refuse the splitter on mixed flags and pass the guard vacuously (2026-10-01 probe work)
Question: which exact check strands the d06 dragon fill, whose single self-crossing loop reaches the fan and denies on work.
Method: stage-boolean stderr tracing through the lowering cascade (reverted), plus refusal-reason tracing at every crossing-splitter exit, on the d06 probe.
Facts: the cascade reports split False, coincident False, touch False, then mixed True with 1 loop out; the splitter refuses at its per-loop purity gate with flags 11 (ZeroLengthEdge plus ProperCrossing plus SelfTouch), so mixed intra-loop defects never enter the split fixpoint. The mixed sweep then finds no actionable sub-operation (no inter-loop crossings on a single loop, no splittable self-touch, no contained, spike, splice, doubled, conform or link step) and breaks through to the guard, which checks loop pairs only and passes a single self-crossing loop vacuously. The fan would compute the correct winding given budget (264M against the 100M ledger), so rewording the denial to simplicity would be dishonest: the geometry is merely expensive, and splitting its crossings would still leave shared-vertex lobes the analytic contact scan refuses, with identical point totals. Two experiments without a mechanism-level improvement; the remaining uncertainty is only quantitative (how many of the 1128 points serve real knot topology versus flattening dust), which changes nothing about the required machinery.
Decision: no product change. The architectural step stands: shared scan-converter membership over touching sets per the active row-4 work order, which admits subdivided stroke unions and split self-crossing fills to analytic traversal with per-probe oracle fringe-equality. Next: the shared-scanner slice itself, inv-065 matrix composition, or row-7 paint work per the queue.

## Triage round: shift, composition and linear-skew ruled out; coincident queue pruned (2026-10-01 probe work)

## Horizontal walker edges snap quarter-up and round half-up; Chrome does neither (2026-10-01 probe work)
Question: why Walker-rendered horizontal edges sit systematically below Chrome while rectangle-band edges at the same phases render exact.
Method: isolate the path (rect bands versus fractional polygon through the walker) at one phase, then run an eighth-phase family of 1px polygon bars (declared upfront: tune k=1,3 with even-k sanity anchors, hold out k=5,7), each with a fresh banked 3/3-stable Chrome triple.
Facts: rectangle bands match Chrome at every eighth phase including exact halves, so the band path is exonerated and the bias lives in the walker. Polygon bars diverge at every fractional phase while integers match: k=1 reads oracle 223/31 against ours 191/64, k=3 reads 159/95 against 128/128, k=5 reads 95/159 against 64/191, k=7 reads 31/223 against 0/255, k=2 reads 191/63 against 191/64, k=4 reads 127/127 against 128/128, k=6 reads 63/191 against 64/191. Every oracle value equals truncated true coverage (223.125 to 223, 127.5 to 127) while ours equal quarter-snapped edges (4.125 walks as 4.25) with round-half-up bytes (127.5 to 128). The arithmetic is exact: SnapY adds one eighth then floors to quarters, so on-grid quarters step up by one eighth, and the trapezoid alpha ops round where Chrome truncates. The declared holdout verifies without further fitting (k=5 predicts effective 4.75/5.75, k=7 predicts vanishing top with full bottom, both observed). Oracle triple SHAs 0f69d9db4c137f60daf4928be51c4dfb0c4072df9f10a520576f7db241d44c3f, 619b56bc5db558164e27029ad53fff40c0127d3d6eb22b230137ce0a441fcd08, 1ae39f18efafc52eb97e2b4938cf75b180cf0b89035dc2a0fe037d23dd9eebe7, f22ae7f295ff5f750589ec5ed5cf34255af575a8f36feaabe6df41e2f9db8fd6, c932bac93bfdf9bf6545efbfb5991ee21dceed74872a9dc4bfe6a3b07e5d676a, 37ee784cdca336327bf996c50932ab927c11ba544794808085a58ae25c0913be, 9c0d5d780c0e15b8e918418b495b538d1cf5892feafb9aa2b2696f4f5696ae0a, 6e3399cfda96ba2dfc15446ad5b613817923b8faff8cd539e6be6ab550e6001b (pinned Chrome 153.0.8010.52 recipe 3, 16x16).
Decision: no product change this turn; the fix (desnap plus truncate-bytes) is a corpus-wide change that needs its own gate, reprobe and ratchet turn. Open design question first: whether Chrome matches unsnapped evaluation or a finer snap grid (16th-phase probes discriminate), and whether our pinned Skia formula predates Chrome 153's Skia (provenance check per R40 before touching the port). Next: answer both, then land the quantization fix with full validation.

## Desnap fixes placement but breaks topology; sixteenth equals desnap on eighths; Chrome uses true fractional trunc (2026-10-01 probe work)
Question: whether Chrome matches unsnapped evaluation or a finer snap grid, whether desnap preserves wordmark topology, what the n56f micro-edge does, and whether the pinned Skia formula predates Chrome 153 backend (open questions from the prior turn).
Method: render clean baselines with the clean Release DLL (Lokad.Svg.dll SHA-256 CAC2E43FBECAC540B2E888DB65C081C2AAC74DBD84295A7AC7479775F00A0876) via MemoryProbe for the wordmark pair, triple, full-native probes, the eighth-phase poly family and the sixteenth-phase snap pair, and compare against banked 3/3-stable oracles with the px decoder; stage a temporary Y-desnap in SetLine (bypass SnapY, X snap kept, bytes still round; reverted) and re-render the same set; compare prior sixteenth-tree poly renders against fresh desnap and oracle; dump n56f raster points through a temporary TRACE (reverted) and scan dust and close pairs; read external/skia SkAnalyticEdge.h and SkAnalyticEdge.cpp at the sparse checkout for SnapY accuracy, setLine branches and row stepping.
Facts: clean baselines reproduce the prior turn bit-for-bit (n56f 151 changed at maximum channel error 86 with maximum alpha error 60 and 2 above 40 against oracle SHA-256 1A4122B51C65440C0279C6962144949FFF40F9A571FFF9E1EFA9B2782F6DAACB; n1718f 168 at 87 with 68 against 6B29206E9D131103DF7670A302CC6F87DF6314EA07C331AC49F4D7A9ECF1713F; n181920f 151 at 86 with 60 and 6 above 40 against 2123EE1130DBAB483086EEF62CCB85F3DCB5F9B3F9A332C04D9022B7972DD5C0; p1native 2023 at 96 with 69 and 159 above 40 against CF8B7F7D0329AB12BF061C62ABC960A9D63BB490C9979997670892D185F47635; poly clean versus oracle 0, 24, 12, 24, 24, 24, 12, 24 for k=0..7; pinned Chrome 153.0.8010.52 recipe 3 throughout). Sixteenth-phase discrimination is decisive: snap16 (4.0625 to 5.0625) reads oracle row4 239 with row5 15 against quarter-snapped candidate 255 with 0 (24 changed pixels over rows 4 to 5; oracle SHA-256 D3692E1ABEFAC5A2C2025BEE6E42C8165444E848A6F99677C056F66D48023F34, 3/3 stable), and snap17 (4.1875 to 5.1875) reads oracle 207 with 47 against candidate 191 with 64 (24 changed pixels; oracle SHA-256 AFC67B4E6820A4A45AD60D67B9D2F202BE9A5AE9123CC6D6150834C19EEF6330, 3/3 stable). Every oracle byte equals truncated true coverage times 255 (0.9375 times 255 is 239.0625 to 239; 0.0625 times 255 is 15.9375 to 15; 0.8125 times 255 is 207.1875 to 207; 0.1875 times 255 is 47.8125 to 47), so Chrome snaps to no grid at any denominator. Temporary desnap confirms placement repair with topology breakage: poly versus oracle moves to 0, 12, 12, 12, 24, 12, 12, 12 (eighths improve 24 to 12, halves hold at 24 from byte rounding alone), while n56f moves 151 to 205 changed with big pixels 2 to 86 at maximum channel error 93 with maximum alpha error 229 (filled notches and holes) and p1native moves 2023 to 5808 changed with big 159 to 4651 at maximum channel error 96 with maximum alpha error 255. Prior sixteenth-tree poly renders read identically to fresh desnap on all eight phases (0, 12, 12, 12, 24, 12, 12, 12), as expected because eighth phases already sit on the sixteenth grid, and prior sixteenth wordmark renders explode the same way (n56f 206 with 71 big; p1native 4469 with 3299 big): grid-size tuning is ruled out, the issue is tie canonicalization, not bias size. The n56f raster dump on the clean tree shows a single 37-point loop with zero dust pairs and exactly one sub-0.3 pair: the consecutive vertical micro-edge 0.0873 at indices 6 to 7 from (40.0285, 33.4167) to (40.0285, 33.3294); the next-smallest consecutive gap is 0.4671, so a 1e-9 bitwise weld would change nothing and the breakage is global stepping, not single-edge dust. WalkEdgesRow stepping assumes quarter-snapped events by construction: the shift logic selects quarter, half and full quanta while GoYShift advances X by DX right-shifted by that quantum, and Skia goY(y, yShift) asserts the step equals Fixed1 right-shifted by yShift; desnapped arbitrary event gaps advance X by the wrong quanta, which is why every wordmark fills holes while isolated bars only shift fringe. Provenance check: the external/skia sparse checkout still carries kDefaultAccuracy 2 quarter SnapY in SkAnalyticEdge.h with setLine SnapY calls in SkAnalyticEdge.cpp, yet Chrome renders true fractional truncation, so Chrome 153 SVG fills do not traverse this pinned walker (different backend); the port predates or bypasses the actual path, and no further source survey follows per the active work order. Byte truncation stays a separate slice and is not bundled: eighths keep 12 pixels at maximum error 1 from rounding versus truncation even with placement fixed, and halves keep 24 for the same reason.
Decision: no product change; all temporaries (TRACE, Y-desnap) are reverted with the tree clean at 6765e54b and Release build at zero warnings. The quantization fix needs its own turn: proportional X stepping for unsnapped event gaps plus truncated bytes, with full gate, 92-document reprobe and ratchet adjudication. Next per the queue: that walker-stepping slice, or row-5 flattening fidelity and row-7 paint work per priorities (triage holds that every remaining big residual outside this slice is curve-edge fringe).


## Box-row bytes truncate while slanted rows keep pinned rounding (2026-10-01 slice)
Question: which byte rule closes the eighth-phase fringe without breaking the 22 diagonal exact fixtures that verify the pinned rounding.

Facts: global truncation graduates the quarter and half eighth-phase bars (k=2, k=4, k=6 read 0 changed pixels against banked triples) and 5 research probes, but regresses 22 exact fixtures by 1 to 13 pixels at maximum error 1 to 3 (diagonal triangles, layer-opacity twins, slanted switches, sliver twins, shared-pixel and two-paint unions) while curve and stroke known-differences wash upward in counts with unchanged maxima: the slanted exact fixtures verify pinned rounding, so global truncation is falsified. Scoped to RectFlow pure-axis-aligned box rows, truncation is unanimous: the same quarter and half bars graduate, the same 5 research probes (fractional-polygon, fractional-path-orthogonal, quantum-tenth-edge, quantum-translucent-127-tenth, fractional-stroke-narrow, previously 8, 8, 12, 12 and 24 changed pixels at maximum error 1) render byte-exact, all 22 diagonal exact fixtures hold byte-identical, and every known-difference fixture holds byte-identical. One research bbox washes (odd-stroke-hairline minimum and maximum mismatch Y move 7 to 8 with counts and maxima unchanged at 12 and 1). Wordmark curves never route RectFlow, so n56f and p1native hold their tuples bit-for-bit through this slice.
Decision: landed box-row truncation with slanted rounding preserved. Next: WalkEdgesRow and SlopeFlow truncation plus proportional stepping for desnapped event gaps (the eighth-phase eighths still need placement), with the same gate, reprobe and ratchet discipline.

## Convex-scope desnap rejected: slanted events break structurally (2026-10-01 probe work)

Circles arrive as 4 quadrant conics (w=sqrt(2)/2) converted to quads at tol 0.25 (pow2=0 for both discs), then forward-differenced (r=4: shift 2, 4 steps per quadrant; r=2: shift 1, 2 steps), with the snap branch never firing on either disc. The prior tiny-edge-quantization bound is superseded as the primary explanation: the walker handles fine segments correctly, it was fed true-ellipse chords instead of conic-quad analytic segments.

## Ford cubic-multi sliver tail attributed to walker combination, not joints (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: whether the 93 Ford sliver regressions from cubic multi substitution sit at span joints, and whether any are visible.
Method: re-enable cubic multi substitution in a scratch worktree only; render Ford 500x200 explicit against the hash-verified pinned oracle; attribute every pixel by old-vs-new error with joint proximity (1.5px) and new-alpha visibility cuts. No product-tree change.
Facts: probe tuple 3958px at max 156/156 versus gated 4192px at max 128/110 (oracle 723F76EE verified); error mass down 40 percent (MAE 0.35093 to 0.20911) with 2835 improved versus 538 regressed, 93 big (delta at least 40) of which 78 are oracle-transparent inventions. Only 2 of 93 sit near span joints, exactly the 2 percent background rate, while 9.8 percent of improved pixels sit near joints: joint handoff is falsified. Visibility: 67 big regressions carry new alpha below 10 (invisible wisps), 5 mid, 21 at alpha 40 or above; worst is (64,192) with oracle alpha 27, base 10, new 183. Cubic edge construction is verified faithful step-for-step, so the tail rides walker-level combination of the denser analytic edge set (close assign-vs-add and smooth-jump decisions around substituted spans).
Decision: cubic multi substitution stays gated with no product change; the probe code never leaves the removed scratch worktree. Next: compare close and smooth-jump decisions old-versus-new around substituted spans, starting at (64,192).

## Blowout shape localized; joint flattening eliminated; segmentation blamed (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: what construction divergence puts the worst Ford sliver a pixel right of the oracle while flattened chords sit a pixel left.
Method: scratch-worktree probes only, no product change: row-shape decode around (64,192), span census of the region, upstream fetch of pinned SkEdgeBuilder.cpp at the release pin, joint-flattening removal, cubic step-doubling.
Facts: rows 189 to 193 show a symmetric one-pixel straddle with the oracle in the middle, an interval-boundary signature. Two shallow cubic runs form that bottom edge. The fetched builder confirms analytic cubics chop at Y extrema, mirroring our chop. FlattenCubicJoint has no pinned provenance and its removal is raster-inert (Ford tuple identical to 15 digits; cubic fixture holds 32px): eliminated, left in product untouched. Doubling cubic steps degrades Ford 3958 to 4039 with mass up, so the shift formula is right and per-piece float inputs diverge on boundary-sensitive shallow pieces: our double-pipeline floats versus Chrome's float pipeline flip segmentation or endpoint quanta.
Decision: cubic multi stays gated. Next: hunt per-piece shift flips on the boundary spans (instrument chopped-piece shift values), then scope the early-float quantization slice.

## Shift flips and tiling falsified; divergence isolated to float inputs (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: whether per-piece shift flips or edge-tiling defects explain the one-pixel blowout shift.
Method: scratch-worktree trace hooks only, no product change: record every chopped cubic piece shift during a cubic-multi Ford render, replicate the shift formula in-test (validated exact on all 169 pieces), perturb each float input by one ulp; separately trace every emitted edge per piece and check Y tiling.
Facts: all 169 pieces reproduce the recorded shift exactly and none flips under ulp perturbation: shift-boundary flips falsified. All 169 pieces tile Y exactly with zero gaps, overlaps, or empty pieces: construction self-consistent, no dropped or doubled coverage. Blowout piece edges dumped on record (row 192: X 67.0 to 64.47). With chop, shift, resampling, slope threading, tiling, and joint handling all verified or eliminated, the only unverified stage is the float inputs themselves: our double-pipeline floats versus Chrome float pipeline, plus chop-t placement.
Decision: cubic multi stays gated. Next: replicate the float pipeline for the two blowout spans (parse-to-float plus viewBox map in float32) and compare against our doubles-turned-float.

## Isolated span exonerates construction; blowout is combination-level (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: whether the Ford blowout comes from cubic edge construction or multi-contour combination.
Method: scratch diagnostics only, no product change and no corpus additions: extract blowout span B standalone with its close chord at identity map, bank a 3/3 byte-identical pinned-Chrome triple, render through the substituted single-contour walker, compare bottom rows.
Facts: isolated span B matches the oracle to within two quanta on every bottom row (rows 187 and 188 byte-identical, row 191 identical, row 192 one pixel 255 to 253, overall max alpha error 19), while the same curve inside Ford blows out 27 to 183. Edge construction is exonerated at piece level; the divergence needs the multi-contour context: strip partitioning through intersections and ordering, close assign-vs-add decisions, and winding intervals across substituted contours.
Decision: cubic multi stays gated. Next: combination-level investigation starting from strip partitions old-versus-new on Ford.

## Strip diff shows same structure with shifted splits; ordering is next (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: whether the blowout comes from strip partitions, close decisions, or crossing order.
Method: scratch-worktree row-192 strip tracing in gated versus cubic-multi Ford renders (identical hooks, worktrees removed after).
Facts: both walks emit 26 strips and 19 blits with zero close=True decisions anywhere on the row, so assign-vs-add is exonerated here. The blowout interval reads rite 63.63 split at 192.5 gated versus rite 66.15 split at 192.75 substituted, with matching left boundaries: only substituted-edge X positions and their LowerY-driven strip splits differ. The rite edge X is isolation-verified, so the remaining suspect is crossing order and winding-interval formation across the sorted active list, not edge placement or strip logic.
Decision: cubic multi stays gated. Next: active-edge ordering trace per strip around the blowout interval.

## Verification ledger closed except mask accumulation; table exact (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: what construction stage remains unverified for the blowout edge.
Method: source-level verification of every remaining artifact against the pinned release, no product change: inverse-table programmatic diff, parse-rounding bit comparison on all blowout coordinates, winding-sign audit of the traced crossings.
Facts: inverse tables match 1025 of 1025 entries; (float)double versus float parse agree bit-exact on all 16 blowout coordinates; transform is identity-exact; traced windings agree in sign on both sides (left minus one, rite plus one) with identical interval structure. Every cubic construction stage is now verified or bounded-negligible except chop-t association values and per-strip mask-row accumulation. The 183 must therefore compose inside the mask walk, not the edges.
Decision: cubic multi stays gated. Next: mask-row accumulation trace for row 192 showing each strip and fill contribution to pixels 60 to 68.

## Mask semantics close composition; bit-compare of span edges is next (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: how the 183 composes, and whether Ford span-B edges equal the isolation-verified ones bit-for-bit.
Method: scratch-worktree mask-op tracing in gated versus cubic-multi Ford renders; fringe-quantum forensics on the banked oracle.
Facts: row 192 runs all-ADD (fractional slabs, close never fires), so coverage is pure strip summation: x64 sums strip-A interior plus strip fringes to 183 while base lacks strip-A coverage there. Oracle fringe steps (237, 178, 122, 67, 27) are not sample-patterned, supporting the analytic backend for Ford. The rite edge X remains the necessary and sufficient difference, yet the same edge matched in isolation: the next experiment dumps both edge lists and compares bit-for-bit to decide between identical-edges-different-context and different-edges.
Decision: cubic multi stays gated. Next: bit-compare Ford versus isolated span-B edges.

## Bisection isolates span B; map verified; paradox stands (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: which contour and span drives the blowout, and whether the device map is fully understood.
Method: scratch-worktree substitution flags with cursor-safe exclusion semantics (a naive StartIndex filter starves span cursors and silently substitutes nothing: void result caught by pixel check), plus bit-exact forward-map verification and file-structure census.
Facts: contour-0-only substitution reproduces the blowout (3968) while counter-only stays base-like (4186); span B is necessary (all-but-B gives 10) and sufficient alone (only-B gives 183) with span A irrelevant (all-but-A gives 183). The element-matrix forward map reproduces retained doubles bit-exact; the file uses only relative m and c commands (no smooth, use, or symbol content), killing the reflection and instance hypotheses. Bisection corroborates the chop extremum; piece engagement counts prove substitution fires (1 synthetic piece, 169 Ford pieces). Yet span-B edges are bit-identical Ford-versus-isolation while outcomes differ 183 versus oracle-match: the same verified edges in different interval neighborhoods.
Decision: cubic multi stays gated. Next: explain how identical edges yield different fringe in different neighborhoods, starting from strip-split weights versus edge positions on span B alone.

## Registration falsified; parse aligned; inverse-fit queued (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: whether the blowout is capture misregistration or parse divergence, and what experiment can break the edge-versus-context deadlock.
Method: shift-search with exact VisualDiff semantics over the banked oracle (PNG unfiltering included); independent exact-arithmetic walk of both Ford paths matched against all 142 retained spans.
Facts: shift-search shows a sharp global minimum at zero offset (4192 versus 5869 nearest), so registration is correct; every retained span matches its independently walked source group, so parse, accumulation, and mapping are self-consistent end to end. The paradox stands in full force: verified-identical edges with verified-identical interval structure produce different fringe in different neighborhoods, which isolates the remaining question to strip-split weights versus edge positions on span B alone.
Decision: cubic multi stays gated. Next: inverse-fit experiment, solving for the rite-edge placement and LowerY split that best explain oracle fringe locally around rows 189 to 193.

## Bisection converges on span B; paint and registration excluded (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: which contour and span drives the blowout, and whether paint or registration contribute.
Method: scratch-worktree substitution flags with cursor-safe exclusion semantics, shift-search with exact comparison semantics, file-structure census, oracle interior sampling.
Facts: contour-0-only substitution reproduces the blowout (3968) while counter-only stays base-like (4186); span B is necessary (all-but-B gives 10) and sufficient alone (only-B gives 183) with span A irrelevant; a naive StartIndex match-time filter starves span cursors and silently substitutes nothing. Registration minima sit exactly at zero offset; the file uses only relative m and c commands with no smooth, use, or symbol content; oracle opaque interior is exactly our blue; the device map reproduces retained doubles bit-exact. Yet span-B edges are bit-identical across renders while Ford overshoots and isolation matches.
Decision: cubic multi stays gated. Next: alpha-op transcription check on analytic inputs, then the inverse-fit on span B if needed.

## Two-alpha mask path is always additive like pinned (2026-10-02 slice)
Question: whether the analytic alpha ops transcribe the pinned release exactly, and what the two-alpha mask branch does on full rows.
Method: line-by-line comparison of trapezoid, triangle, partial-alpha, single, two, full, and wide-row operations plus call-site argument order against the pinned release; reachability probe for the one divergent branch.
Facts: everything matches except BlitTwoAlphas, which assigned on full rows without close edges where pinned always adds; arg orders, single/full/wide paths, and saturation all agree. A firing counter over wordmarks, curve fixtures, and dense synthetics reads 549 full-row close-free two-alpha calls with zero landing on nonzero mask bytes, so the assign branch never observably differs there. Full gate holds 2804 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font green with zero warnings and zero drift; memory and package checks pass; paired Ford and inv-001 renders are byte-identical old-versus-new.
Decision: landed as pinned-parity hardening with zero measured effect. The Ford sliver tail is unaffected (wrong direction: this heals under-coverage only).

## Closing-compacted spans drop instead of anchor-snapping (2026-10-02 slice)
Question: whether the Ford blowout rides the weld-remap anchor snap shortening span B, and how to re-anchor spans without fabricating chords.
Method: adjudicate drop-versus-snap by endpoint provenance (interior drops sit within epsilon of the probed anchor; closing drops sit near the contour start while the probe lands on the previous vertex); mark the two drops distinctly in the weld index map; census Ford spans old-versus-new plus paired renders on both wordmarks.
Facts: a first blanket drop of every weld-dropped endpoint broke the committed interior remap test (cubic starting on a 4e-10 dust vertex), proving interior snaps faithful and forcing the closing-only split via the WeldClosingDrop mark. Ford census: 142 to 140 cubic spans with only the two closing-snapped spans dropped (contour-0 span B [270,277] sitting in the (64,192) sliver neighborhood, plus one second-fill closing span [293,296]) and all other 140 spans byte-identical including indices, so no retained span can mismatch its chord by more than 1e-9 anymore. Ford Default/500x200-explicit and inv-001 Default renders hold byte-identical old-versus-new (SHAs 17BED3B5, D29DF34A, D578E9E1), keeping the 4192px and 1128px oracle tuples. Full gate holds 2806 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font green with zero warnings; memory and package checks pass with zero .svg in the package.
Decision: landed with two closing-dust span tests (both fail pre-fix with two retained spans, pass post-fix with the earlier span kept) and a repurposed closing-map lock; the committed interior remap tests stand unmodified. Cubic multi substitution stays gated: the fabricated-chord mechanism is structurally gone, but enablement needs its own walker slice with a fresh Ford re-probe. Next: cubic multi-contour substitution on top of this remap, then the queued de Casteljau-correct controls and early-float quantization follow-ups.
## Cubic spans substitute per contour in the multi walker (2026-10-02 slice)

Full 92-document Default re-probe: 88 render plus 4 reject both trees with zero disposition flips and 81 byte-identical including every exact entry; 11 known-difference movers drift at fringe scale (worst MAE 0.21 on Ford Default, all others at or below 0.12 with zero alpha error on 7 of 11). Oracle direction measured everywhere size-matched: inv-057 5869 to 5788, inv-064 27993 to 27983, inv-065 holding 12080 with MAE down, inv-081 5199 to 4119 with max 95 to 78, inv-082 holding 27240; the remaining five movers have no size-matched banked oracle at Default so their tuples stand as dated records.
