## Span retention verified across the admitted device maps; skew stays honestly gated (2026-10-02 checkpoint)
Question: whether span retention covers the complete admitted device transform, and where the honest admission boundary sits.
Method: census spans under rotate, skew, reflection, non-uniform scale, and rotated-cubic maps at parse level; read the BuildDeviceMap gate and the frame-scale curve gate.
Facts: rotation, reflection, and non-uniform element scales all retain exact affine-mapped spans (Bezier form is affinely invariant, so the device map needs no per-transform span logic); skewX rejects at lowering with the uniform-scale message and non-uniform frame maps reject curve fills at rasterization, both pre-existing honest gates owned by row-7 admission and mapping work. Four locks pin the boundary: exact quad control/endpoint mapping through non-uniform scale and reflection, exact cubic control mapping through an integer quarter-turn matrix, and the skew rejection code-plus-message. Full gate holds 2811 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font green with zero warnings; memory and package checks pass with zero .svg in the package.
Decision: recorded as the row-5 transform checkpoint with no product change; the retained-curve gap as scoped is closed (retention, both walkers, resampling, instance carry, weld remap, cubic multi, transform coverage, Ford wordmark progress). Row 5 reopens only if skew or general-matrix admission lands (spans should flow through the new maps by affinity, with twin locks then) or the non-uniform frame gate lifts. Next per the queue is the row-4 quantization slice (proportional X stepping plus truncated bytes) or row-6 curve-aware strokes.
## Quad and cubic dash probes isolate dash-cut divergence from outline error (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: whether curve-dash oracle residuals come from dash cuts (phase/tangents) or the underlying stroke outline, and whether true tangents have a measurable target.
Method: two 16x16 dashed curve probes (quad arch, cubic S with inflection, dasharray 4 3) plus two solid-stroke controls of the same geometry; fresh 3/3-stable triples on the hash-verified pinned browser; candidate renders compared with VisualDiff.
Facts: quad dash reads 46px at max 205/205 against its solid control at 38px at max 32/32, so the dash-specific component dominates with high maxima; cubic dash reads 41px at max 134/134 against its solid control at 31px at max 70/70, a mixed picture. No quad or cubic dash had ever been oracle-measured before; all five standing dash ratchets are circle-sourced (no spans possible). All four triples are 3/3 byte-identical. Full gate holds 2811 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font green with zero warnings; memory and package checks pass with zero .svg in the package.
Decision: admitted as four research ratchets with no product change; the dash probes motivate the queued true-tangent slice while the solid controls are its hold-baseline (a tangent change must not move them). Next: attribute the dash residual split (cut position along the curve versus cap angle at cuts) before threading retained spans into dash-cut tangents.
## Dash residual split attributed: phase exonerated, tangents convicted (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: whether the quad/cubic dash residuals come from cut position (arc-length phase) or cap angle (cut tangents).
Method: oracle-versus-candidate overlay maps (blue oracle-only, red candidate-only) for both dash probes; flattened-versus-quadrature arc-length measurement on both curves; span census on the cubic probe contour.
Facts: both overlays show bodies aligned with ends displaced about one pixel along the tangent (blue behind, red ahead at successive cuts), the phase signature, yet quad flattened length trails analytic by only 0.04px on a 7px dash period, far below what 205-maxima pixels need, so phase is exonerated and the residual is tangent-class by elimination with solid controls bounding outline error far below (38 at max 32, 31 at max 70). The cubic probe contour self-touches (S-curve crosses its closing chord), so its span drops even on the fill route and its cuts fall back to chordal tangents on rewritten points. Spans are not retained on the stroke route at all, so true tangents need retention enablement plus cut-to-parameter mapping there, not just a tangent formula swap.
Decision: no product change; the threading slice is greenlit with a scoped design (enable retention on the stroke builder with reservation accounting, thread spans to DashEndpointTangent, map cut edge-plus-fraction to curve parameter, evaluate analytic tangents with chordal fallback, hold solid controls). Next: implement the threading slice against the dash ratchets.

## Dash corner turns adjudicated: joins-alone and tangents-alone both exonerated (2026-10-02 probes)
Question: whether dash-run corner turns (open-branch joins) or cut tangents drive the closed-dash 205-maxima.
Method: open-versus-closed and offset-5 (seam-gap, no merge) oracle comparisons; closedLoop join threading through EmitJoin; analytic cut tangents via retained spans with chordal fallback; per-pixel worst lists before and after each change.
Facts: open dashes match at 55 max while closed dashes read 205 (offset 0) and 233 (offset 5, no merge possible), isolating corners and the chord; closed solid strokes with the same corners match at 32 max. ClosedLoop joins move 33px at max 107 but the ratchets go 46 at 205 to 47 at 211 and 41 at 134 to 40 at 141, sideways-to-worse. Analytic tangents move one pixel (46 to 45, 41 to 40) with maxima pinned and corner pixels byte-identical (12,12 holds 229/24), proving chordal-parabola already exact at these cuts. Both changes reverted; the tree is back to the gated state.
Decision: neither mechanism alone explains the residual, so no threading lands; the remaining suspect is corner-turn run geometry as a coupled system (join style plus adjacent cut caps) or the chord-run phase convention, both requiring run-layout instrumentation rather than another blind swap. Next per the queue is row-6 stroke completion on measured items or the row-4 quantization slice, not a third dash experiment.

## Gap centers hold: closed-dash divergence localizes to chord and seam runs (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: whether our dash cuts sit at nominal pattern positions, and where exactly the closed-dash residual lives.
Method: sample our and oracle renders at nominal gap centers (offset 0 and offset 5) plus row-11/12 decimal dumps; hand-verify run intervals against pattern math.
Facts: all nominal gap centers agree (both empty) on curve and chord, so cuts match nominally and uniform phase error is impossible (loop lengths agree to 0.05px anyway); the seam merge is excluded by offset 5 reading 233 max with a gap forced at the seam. The worst pixels all touch the chord or seam: (12,12) oracle 229 versus ours 24 sits 0.24px past our s=18 cut, and (2,11) oracle 255 versus ours 95 sits on overlapped head-plus-tail ink, implicating chord-run outline narrowing or seam overlap composition rather than cut placement. Chord ink extents differ by about a pixel downstream while curve ink matches.
Decision: no product change; the prescription sharpens to run-level instrumentation (dump our actual run intervals and outline vertex counts on chord and seam, compare against nominal) before any outline or convention change. Next: instrument dash runs, then fix from observed layout.

## Dash run layout certified nominal; outline observation prescribed (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: whether our dash cuts sit at nominal pattern positions, observed directly rather than inferred from pixels.
Method: temporary run-layout hook in StrokeDashRun recording cut segments, fractions, arc positions, and point counts (reverted after); nominal pattern math for offset 0 on the 28.24 loop.
Facts: observed runs are [7,11], [14,18], [21,25], and merged [28,28.24]+[0,4] with arc positions exact to printed precision and point counts 4, 3, 2, 4 matching cut-plus-corner structure. Cuts are nominal, so the residual lives purely in run outlining (caps, joins, winding) on chord and seam runs. A stale-scratch caution: overwritten candidate PNGs are untrustworthy for attribution; only contemporaneous aggregates rule.
Decision: no product change; the hook is reverted with the tree clean. Next: dump run outline vertices (not just intervals) on chord and seam runs versus nominal outline geometry, then fix from observed outlines.

## Dash-cut tangents clamp to the cut edge at sharp corners (2026-10-02 slice)
Question: whether the tilted caps from corner-spanning Lagrange parabolas explain the closed-dash corner residual.
Method: outline-vertex observation of the turning run (cap edge slashes instead of standing vertical); exact hand computation of the parabola derivative at the corner cut; clamp DashEndpointTangent to the exact cut edge direction past forty-five degrees of disagreement.
Facts: the turning run outline shows the end cap slashing from (11.26,11.81) to (13.22,12.19) instead of standing at x=12.24, and the hand computation gives a parabola tangent of (-0.25,0.97) against the chord (-1,0) at the s=18 cut, a seventy-five-degree error from fitting across the 120-degree corner with 1:8 legs. With the clamp, quad dash moves 46px at max 205 to 44px at max 159 and cubic dash 41px at max 134 to 38px at max 70, both ratchets re-measured; solids hold. Full 92-document re-probe shows zero drift with byte-identical renders on both trees. Full gate holds 2811 core plus 100 visual plus 6 font green with zero warnings; memory and package checks pass with zero .svg in the package.
Decision: landed as the corner-tangent clamp with re-measured dash ratchets. Next: the remaining dash maxima (159, 70) and the run-join question re-open only with outline-level evidence.

## Dash run outlines self-cancel at sharp-turn folds; two-loop emission prescribed (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: why closed-dash corners lose ink when cuts, joins, tangents, and phase are each exonerated or neutral.
Method: temporary outline-vertex dumps (reverted) of the turning and seam runs; exact winding numbers at disputed pixels; solid-outline dump of the same contour for comparison.
Facts: the merged seam run outline zigzags (2,11) to spike tip (3.90,11) back to (2.82,12.57), doubling back on itself; winding at (2.5,11.5) and (3.5,11.5) reads exactly 0 where ink belongs, while the solid two-loop outline covers the same region through its independent backward loop. Single-loop forward-plus-backward concatenation cancels spike folds under nonzero fill; the solid twin survives by loop redundancy. Sharpness scaling explains rect-dash exactness (90-degree folds cancel sub-pixel) versus quad-corner holes (120-degree folds cancel pixels). ClosedLoop joins re-tested on top of correct caps move nothing measurable, confirming the branch is irrelevant to folds.
Decision: no product change; hook and flip reverted with the tree clean. Next: emit closed-loop dash runs as two independent loops (forward-chain loop plus backward-chain loop, each closed) mirroring solid structure, with rect-dash exactness as the no-regression guard; gentle turns must render byte-identical.

## Spike folds cancel in single loops; ear-redundancy versus bevel weighed (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: why winding cancels at dash spike folds while solid twins cover, and what fix preserves both.
Method: chain-assignment tracing of the merged run outline against solid forward and backward loops; winding arithmetic on the fold edges; red-team of the two-loop prescription against open runs.
Facts: the dash chain visits (2,11), spike tip (3.90,11), then (2.82,12.57) and dives to (4.32,10.38), while the solid forward chain shares the identical fold and cancels identically; solid survives only through its independent backward loop covering x=3.5 across y 8.37 to 13. Two-loop emission as literally stated fails for open runs (the straight closing edge invents coverage), so the viable candidates narrow to emitting spike-ear triangles as independent loops (idempotent where already right via nonzero fill, fixing where canceled, but coincident-merge machinery may interact) or beveling sharp spikes (contradicts solid spike agreement unless scoped to dash runs). Rect-dash exactness holds either way at 90 degrees.
Decision: no product change; tree clean. Next: implement ear-loop emission for sharp dash-run spikes with rect-dash byte-identity as the guard, falling back to scoped bevel if merge machinery interferes; gentle turns must render byte-identical.
## Spike-ear loops falsified in four variants; dash thread parked (2026-10-02 probes)
Question: whether redundant ear triangles can cover fold-cancellation holes without side effects.
Method: four ear variants measured against the dash ratchets with per-pixel worst lists: unsigned, centroid-orientation-matched, loop-orientation fixed-order, and hole-gated consensus.
Facts: unsigned ears erase inked neighbors by opposition (cubic 70 to 162 max); centroid orientation is ill-posed on boundary-hugging triangles; fixed-order ears leave quad at 46 at 163 with cubic unchanged; hole-gated consensus vetoes on grazed samples so seam holes stay empty while cubic holds 38 at 70. No variant improves on the clamp-only baseline (44 at 159, 38 at 70); each trades one pixel cluster for another. All ear code reverted with the tree clean.
Decision: the dash residual stands as rated; ear redundancy cannot distinguish holes from fringe without per-corner join knowledge neither engine exposes at this boundary. Next per the queue is row-6 measured stroke items, row-4 quantization with a proven premise, or row-7 blockers; dash corners re-open only on a join-treatment oracle probe that separates miter, bevel, and clip per corner.

## Throughput evidence: analytic substitution pays off on curve wordmarks (2026-10-02 measurement)
Question: whether the row-4/5 walker, span, resampling, and weld work regressed throughput, since ledger counts alone are not throughput evidence.
Method: fresh BenchmarkDotNet ShortRun of SvgCurveBenchmarks and SvgInventoryBenchmarks on the same box, SDK, and runtime as the banked baselines (Sept 27 curve, Oct 1 inventory).
Facts: ConvertOutlinedWordmark falls 1449 to 607 microseconds (2.4 times faster) with allocations collapsing 5965 to 273 kilobytes (22 times less) and Gen0/Gen1/Gen2 collections falling 1246 each to 64/2/0; gradient clip and isolated opacity hold (58 and 50 microseconds, allocations flat). Inventory shapes hold within ShortRun noise: tiny mark 32 to 35 microseconds, wordmark pair 1972 to 2368 microseconds with overlapping wide intervals on a GC-heavy 2ms op, dense dragon 253 to 267 milliseconds with allocation profiles flat (3026 to 3079 kilobytes, identical Gen counts). No benchmark regresses in allocation on any input.
Decision: recorded as throughput evidence with no product change; ShortRun N=3 stays the comparison protocol and a longer run is unwarranted without a specific regression signal. Next per the queue as before.

Postscript: an intermediate full-gate run showed one unexplained visual failure that never reproduced across two subsequent full runs (full gate plus isolated visual suite, sequential execution); concurrent dotnet processes from parallel probe and memory scripts are the suspected confound, and any recurrence must be investigated as test isolation rather than assumed flaky.

## Oracle 180-degree asymmetry floors symmetric renderers on diagonals (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: whether inv-085 white-stroke fringe is width, offset, byte-rule, or placement error, and what bounds it.
Method: edge-profile means (flat zero everywhere, killing width and offset); per-pixel byte-rule vote on exact-area coverages (440 to 502 tie, killing the byte rule); coverage inversion (ours matches true geometry, oracle reads 0.08 low one-sided); outline symmetry probe (exact to 1e-15, killing kernel asymmetry); 180-degree symmetry audit of both renders.
Facts: the oracle carries 54506 asymmetry mass over 5666 pixels against ours at 74 over 74; our error rises monotonically with oracle asymmetry (0.01 on symmetric pixels, 19.6 at asymmetry 32) while symmetric fringe matches at 0.01 mean; the symmetric-error floor is 57 percent of our residual mass with floor max 24 against our max 32. The asymmetry envelope is smooth and mirror-symmetric (no capture tiling), implicating renderer-side magnitude-dependent rounding in Chrome Skia, which our fixed-point pipeline does not reproduce.
Decision: no product change; the symmetric component is already exact and the remainder needs backend-quirk fidelity outside any bounded slice (the actual backend is not the pinned walker per the provenance finding). Milestone exactness on symmetric documents may need a quirk budget; that is a release decision, not an implementation task. Next per the queue as before.
## White-stroke fringe localized to snap-phase placement, red and width exonerated (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: what drives inv-085 unclipped white-stroke fringe given exact synthetic diagonal coverage.
Method: white-only isolation render (byte-identical to full at all worst spots, exonerating red, clip, cross, and overlap); edge transects both sides; outline symmetry probe; phase-binned error profile.
Facts: white-only matches full byte-for-byte at worst spots; the far edge matches to 5 levels while the near edge sits 0.21px outside oracle; outline offsets are symmetric to 1e-15; the error profile is flat zero with localized 30-maxima at knife-edge phases on both edges. The residual is snap-phase placement visible only at knife phases, plus the oracle-asymmetry floor: no width, byte-rule, red-fringe, or kernel defect.
Decision: no product change; tree clean. The fringe joins the parked desnap-topology research with a second independent confirmation (synthetic probes stay exact because their phases avoid knife edges). Next per the queue as before.

## Quadratic-fixture remainder attributed to single-quantum walker fringe (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: whether the 24px quadratic-fixture remainder rides tessellation placement or controls (row 5) versus walker quantization (row 4).
Method: a temporary deleted-after test rendering logo-quadratic-mark at 64x64 transparent with a per-pixel oracle-versus-candidate dump plus nearest-partial distance; scratch output deleted with the test.
Facts: all 24 changed pixels are themselves partial (nearest-partial distance 0) in symmetric left/right flank pairs; 22 differ by exactly plus-or-minus 1 alpha with the running edges reading 10-to-16 and 128-to-127/126 class pairs, and 2 apex pixels read oracle-transparent versus candidate alpha 2 (the 255 channel maximum is transparent-RGB normalization, alpha error at most 6, matching the ratchet to all digits). Zero interior or topology pixels.
Decision: no product change; row-5 tessellation and controls stand exonerated on this fixture and the remainder sits at the quantum floor of the pinned fixed-point walker, so slanted-quantization changes stay gated behind the 22 diagonal exact fixtures. Temp test and scratch deleted with the tree clean. Next per the queue is the row-4 quantization slice with a proven premise or row-6 measured stroke items, not another curve-placement probe.

## Transparent-stop premultiplication dithers around Bayer cell centers (2026-10-02 slice)
Question: whether the transparent-probe oracle residuals come from the dither threshold, the unpremultiplication order, or gradient geometry.
Method: exact float64 replica of the transparent pipeline (position, blend, alpha, premult floor, unpremult round) checked byte-for-byte against our candidate, then scored against both banked oracle triples under candidate dither rules.
Facts: the replica matches our candidate on all 512 pixels, so geometry and ordering are exact; the centered rule (matrix + 0.5) / 64 reproduces both oracles byte-for-byte (the old rule misses exactly the 1- and 4-pixel admitted tuples at cells whose fractional sum sits 0.005 below the flip boundary). A third multistop probe falls exact under the same rule with no new mechanism. On inventory composites the same rule trades sub-visible quanta (inv-035 radial 58 improved versus 307 regressed at max 2, inv-041 sheen 10 versus 190 at unchanged maxima, zero above-40 anywhere); a mask-255 opaque-tile routing changes neither attribution digit and is reverted as unproven. The gradient-fade fixture collapses 62 pixels at max 128 to 44 at max 1 as the amplified-quantum win.
Decision: landed as a scoped TransparentTileColor change with three exact graduations and one re-measured ratchet; composite-context dither phase re-opens only with a tile-level (pre-composite) oracle discriminator. Next per the queue is row-4 quantization with a proven premise or row-6 measured stroke items.

## Endpoint arcs walk kappa-cubic spans; degenerate rounded rects stay legacy (2026-10-02 slice)
Question: whether the rounded-rectangle 20-pixel max-89 residual rides arc tessellation density, and whether analytic substitution closes it without moving unrelated routes.
Method: per-pixel oracle-versus-candidate dump (all 20 pixels self-partial symmetric arc-fringe pairs, systematic under-paint); analytic segment census (flatness 0.25 stops each corner at 2 segments with 0.23px chord sagitta); walker substitution check with and without spans on the lowered payload; exact-area supersampling as ground truth.
Facts: substitution is live with exact kappa controls and moves the probe to 20 at max 44 on the same pixels; exact area sits between the walker (under) and the oracle (over), so the remainder is coverage-model over-coverage, not geometry. A degenerate half-disc inventory render first regressed 154 versus 9 improved with a flat run at the shared-center touching joint, adjudicating a structural degenerate gate: degenerate rects keep legacy order with no retention and render byte-identical, while the three non-degenerate rounded-mark fixtures improve counts with alpha maxima 77 to 44. Full-ellipse loops and beyond-quarter arcs record nothing by design.
Decision: landed as arc span recording plus rounded-rect carry-through with the degenerate gate, five tests and three re-measured ratchets plus one research tuple; RRect-versus-arcs and walker over-coverage stay queued to row 4/7 with these pixels as discriminators. Next per the queue is row-4 quantization with a proven premise or row-6 measured stroke items.

## Full-loop cubic tiling diverges in walker combination; ellipse spans reverted (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: whether kappa-cubic spans extend from endpoint arcs to full-ellipse loops with the same fidelity.
Method: quarter kappa spans over unchanged ellipse samples with exact controls and full-loop tiling (atan2 end-angle partial span for the closing quarter, close chord unchanged); per-quarter substitution bisection; circle-mark worst-pixel quadrant distribution; exact-area supersampling as ground truth.
Facts: substitution is live (143 differing bytes) with textbook controls and exact tiling, yet circle/ellipse marks regress substantially with bipolar plus-or-minus 80 errors spread evenly across all quadrants and quarters, while the sparse-span arc slice improves; exact area sits between walker and oracle on arc fringe as before, so placement is exonerated and the contrast isolates combination on fully-tiled loops. A density audit shows the pinned stepper runs shift 1 to 3 on small curves (2 to 8 steps, comparable to chord sampling), so substitution value is density-relative and fringe reshuffles at matched density.
Decision: no product change; fully reverted with the tree clean and the HEAD gate re-confirmed. Full-ellipse substitution stays queued behind walker combination work for fully-tiled cubic loops, with the partial-span tiling design recorded; the small-curve stepper floor is a separate queued lever, not a silent tweak. Next per the queue is row-4 quantization with a proven premise or row-6 measured stroke items.

## Dash-run outer corners miter; bevel and round clip the tips (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: which join treatment Chrome applies at sharp dash-run corners, to replace the falsified ear-redundancy direction.
Method: solid bevel and round variants of the closed-dash quad geometry with fresh 3/3-stable triples on the hash-verified pinned browser; three-way oracle comparison (dash versus solid miter/bevel/round) plus our renders of each.
Facts: our solid bevel and round match their oracles at 37 at max 40 and 39 at max 35; the three oracle treatments agree at 38 of 44 dash-changed pixels and separate only at six corner-tip pixels, where oracle-dash equals oracle-miter within 1 level while bevel and round read 0 or trail by 30. Chrome miters dash-run outer corners; the holes are missing miter spikes from single-loop fold cancellation, not a bevel/clip convention.
Decision: admitted as two join calibration probes with no product change; the dash ratchets stand. Next is outlining each dash run as an independent solid-style open stroke with miter joins at outer corners and dash caps at cuts, guarded by rect-dash byte-identity and the solid controls.

## Dash holes are single-loop bays; tangents, phase and direction exonerated (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: where exactly the dash outline folds relative to the solid outline, and which of tangents, phase, or direction drives it.
Method: temporary reverted hook recovering run centerlines with cuts and custom tangents; sealed-outline dumps for dash (4 loops, 43 points) and solid (2 loops, 20 points); per-contour winding at hole pixels; chord-tangent and reversed-traversal outline variants in-harness; full dashoffset sweep against the banked oracle.
Facts: the hole pixels sit in a bay of the seam-run single loop (two opposing V crossings at one x, net zero) while solid winds minus one through outer-loop redundancy; chord caps fold identically apart from the degenerate vertex pixel; the offset sweep is globally optimal at zero with analytic length trailing by 0.04; reversal folds identically. The corner miter tip matches solid exactly, so joins and tangents stand; the bay opens between ear-return and continuation with the start cap missing by 0.11 pixels on a run starting 0.24 before the sharp corner.
Decision: no product change; hook and temps reverted with the tree clean. Next is the architectural step: dash-run outer-loop redundancy mirroring solid-closed structure, with the closing-edge invention as the explicit design problem to solve first.

## Dash single-loops cannot cover bays; redundancy is required (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: which construction covers the fold-bay pixels that every single-loop variant leaves empty. Method: outline-vertex tracing of the seam run (stub 0.24 before a 124-degree corner, tilted start cap, miter tip, V return); ray-crossing audit per variant (custom, chord, reversed, tip-only mental model); analytic loop-length quadrature (28.2836 true versus 28.24 flattened); exhaustive dashoffset sweep; winding at hole pixels for each variant.
Facts: every single-loop variant nets zero at the holes by topology (up-then-down spike pairs always cancel; removing vertices never adds the missing western boundary; the start cap misses by 0.11). The hole pixel sits 0.13 inside the stroke band, and oracle covers the full corner pixel, so only a second loop passing west (solid-outer style) can cover it. Origin, tangents, joins, direction, and phase are all exonerated together; the stub/corner interplay is necessary but not sufficient to explain coverage.
Decision: no product change; hook and temps reverted with the tree clean and byte-verified. Next is the architectural step proper: dash-run outer-loop redundancy mirroring solid-closed two-loop structure, with the closing-edge invention as the explicit first design problem (it defeated literal two-loop emission for open runs).

## Dash bays need western boundary only redundancy provides (2026-10-02 probe)
Question: which single-loop vertex choice covers the bay, if any. Method: chord-tangent and reversed-traversal outline variants in-harness with winding at hole pixels; ray-crossing audit per variant; cap-position analysis; analytic loop-length quadrature; exhaustive offset sweep.
Facts: every variant nets zero by topology (spike pairs cancel regardless of butt vertices, caps, direction, or phase); the hole needs boundary west of the start cap which misses by 0.11 while the pixel sits 0.13 inside the band; oracle covers the full corner pixel, so only solid-outer-style redundancy qualifies. Origin, tangents, joins, direction, phase, and vertex surgery are jointly exonerated.
Decision: no product change; hook and temps reverted with the tree clean. Next is the architectural step proper: dash-run outer-loop redundancy mirroring solid-closed two-loop structure, with the closing-edge invention as the explicit first design problem.

## Straight stroke outlines unite through shared separation with three abutment guards (2026-10-02 slice)

Each guard adjudicated by a dedicated old-versus-new reproduction (inv-008 180px break/restore, butt 4px break/restore, seam 1px break/restore, polygon-dashed bowtie twin split/repair).
Facts: inv-008, orthogonal-butt and dash-seam render byte-exact; miter-join-acute falls 212px at max 24 to 136 at max 7; fractional-stroke-closed (1px) and odd-stroke-closed (4px) graduate to exact fixtures; round-join 26 to 16, fractional-round-join 58 to 20 with max 15 to 27, odd-round-cap 16 to 15; stroke-round-mark 78 to 39, gradient-use 117 to 81, radial-use 121 to 76, pattern-use 51 to 16 with alpha errors gone; inv-009 3819 to 3548, inv-057 5788 to 5016, inv-070 2016 to 1888; structure census shows wins living in mixed (miter, round joins, inv-008), touch (closed rings, use outlines) and pass-through (round caps) with harm isolated to crossing/touch subdivision of exact abutments; use-instance outline starts rotate coverage-neutrally with byte-identical renders; Ford renders byte-identical old-versus-new.
Decision: landed with three regression tests (duplicate union, crossing-contained guard, curved legacy lock), repinned placement starts, re-measured ratchets and two graduations. Next: dashed-run union behind row-6 dash completion with outline-level evidence; row-5 de Casteljau controls and early-float quantization stay queued.

## Stroke-slice throughput shows no regression signal; overlapping-bands hotspot is pre-existing (2026-10-02 measurement)
Question: whether the stroke-union slice regresses straight-stroke throughput, which had no benchmark coverage.
Method: new SvgStrokeBenchmarks (overlapping bands, multi-contour strokes, dashed control, miter V) run under ShortRun N=3 on the same box, SDK and runtime, pre-slice worktree versus post-slice tree; memory allowances separately gated by the probe suite.
Facts: pre 308.7ms plus or minus 53ms, 5.67ms, 207us, 214us versus post 335.2ms plus or minus 184ms, 5.98ms, 289us, 266us; every delta sits inside overlapping intervals with the identical-code dashed path itself moving within noise, so no regression signal. The 300ms overlapping-bands magnitude on both trees is a pre-existing hotspot independent of the slice.
Decision: recorded as throughput exit evidence with the benchmark kept as a lasting asset; a longer run is unwarranted without a specific signal. The pre-existing hotspot is noted as a future lever, not slice work.

## Stroke-union sweep grind repaired by bypassing overlap stages for strokes (2026-10-02 slice)
Question: why sixteen overlapping rect strokes parse 315ms versus 0.2ms pre-slice when every resolver returns false.
Method: cap-hit census with temporary deleted-after instrumentation (coincident cap hit repeatedly plus crossing once, touch and mixed clean), then per-resolver win attribution from the earlier structure census.
Facts: the coincident sweep ground its cap on the 32-loop set while returning false, buying legacy-equivalent tiling; no measured stroke win resolves through crossing subdivision or coincident splicing (wins live in touch, mixed-contact with ProperCrossing, or pass-through), so strokes bypass both stages and enter mixed only with crossings. Parse falls 315ms to 0.6ms; stroke benchmarks move 335.2ms to 18.0ms with the rest flat-or-better. Full gate holds 2819 core plus 100 visual green with zero ratchet movement; inventory tuples hold to all digits; memory, package and font-probe checks pass.
Decision: landed with remarks. The ShortRun comparison that missed this inside GC noise stands corrected: direct warmed in-process scaling is the reliable instrument for grind-class regressions, with ShortRun retained for protocol numbers.

## Sweep-gate overreach corrected by uncapped census; gates match resolved domains (2026-10-02 slice)
